### Summary: Complete Data Flow

```
EspoCRM Contact.phoneNumber (phone field type, multi-value, returns primary)
  |
  v
WhatsAppCampaignService::resolveAudience() 
  calls $contact->get('phoneNumber') -- gets primary phone string
  |
  v
WhatsAppCampaignService::normalizePhone() 
  strips non-digits, adds +55 for Brazilian, returns E.164 string
  |
  v
WhatsAppCampaignContact.phoneNumber (varchar(50), stored as E.164)
  |
  v
ProcessWhatsAppCampaignChunk::processContacts()
  reads $campaignContact->get('phoneNumber')
  |
  v
ChatwootApiClient::findOrCreateContact($phoneNumber)
  searches Chatwoot API by phone, creates if not found
  expects E.164 format (+5511987654321)
  sends as 'phone_number' in Chatwoot API payload
```

**Key finding**: There is NO WhatsApp JID or LID concept anywhere in the Contact or ChatwootContact entities. The system operates purely on E.164 phone numbers. The `identifier` field on ChatwootContact stores Chatwoot's generic contact identifier (which may vary by channel type), and `sourceId` on ChatwootContactInbox stores the per-inbox contact identifier -- but neither is explicitly typed as a WhatsApp JID or LID.

### 1. ChatwootContactInbox Controller -- Access Validation, Platform Resolution, Entity Creation

**File:** `/home/antl3x/repos/monostax/mono/components/crm/source/custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Controllers/ChatwootContactInbox.php`

**Route:** `POST /ChatwootContactInbox/:id/createConversation` (defined in `routes.json` line 148)

#### Access Validation Pattern (lines 59-88):

```php
// Step 1: Get current user's ChatwootUser (linked by assignedUserId)
$currentUserId = $this->getUser()->getId();
$chatwootUser = $entityManager
    ->getRDBRepository('ChatwootUser')
    ->where(['assignedUserId' => $currentUserId])
    ->order('createdAt', 'ASC')
    ->findOne();

if (!$chatwootUser) {
    throw new Forbidden("You do not have access to this account.");
}

// Step 2: Get the account ID from the entity being acted upon
$inboxAccountId = $contactInbox->get('chatwootAccountId');

// Step 3: Verify membership in that account
$membership = $entityManager
    ->getRDBRepository('ChatwootAccountUserMembership')
    ->where([
        'chatwootUserId' => $chatwootUser->getId(),
        'chatwootAccountId' => $inboxAccountId,
    ])
    ->findOne();

if (!$membership) {
    throw new Forbidden("You do not have access to this account.");
}
```

#### Platform URL / API Key Resolution Pattern (lines 115-143):

```php
// From the ChatwootAccount entity:
$chatwootAccount = $entityManager->getEntityById('ChatwootAccount', $inboxAccountId);
$accountApiKey   = $chatwootAccount->get('apiKey');
$externalAccountId = (int) $chatwootAccount->get('chatwootAccountId');

// From the linked ChatwootPlatform entity:
$platformId = $chatwootAccount->get('platformId');
$platform    = $entityManager->getEntityById('ChatwootPlatform', $platformId);
$platformUrl = $platform->get('backendUrl');
```

The chain is: **ChatwootContactInbox -> chatwootAccountId -> ChatwootAccount -> platformId -> ChatwootPlatform -> backendUrl**

// Inbox external ID (directly on entity, is already the Chatwoot int):

$data = [
    'name'                   => $name,                                     // "2026-03-23 - John Doe"

    'chatwootAccountId'      => $inboxAccountId,                          // EspoCRM entity ID (link)

### 3. ChatwootApiClient::createContact() Response Structure

**File:** `/home/antl3x/repos/monostax/mono/components/crm/source/custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Services/ChatwootApiClient.php`

#### createContact() Method (lines 576-617):

- **Endpoint:** `POST /api/v1/accounts/{accountId}/contacts`
- **Required field:** `inbox_id` (validated on line 583)
- **Input payload fields:** `inbox_id`, `phone_number`, `name` (and any other Chatwoot contact fields)
- **Returns:** `$response['body']` -- the raw decoded JSON body

#### Response Parsing in findOrCreateContact() (lines 2268-2303):

This is the key code that reveals the **Chatwoot API response structure** for `createContact`:

```php
$newContact = $this->createContact($platformUrl, $accountApiKey, $accountId, $contactData);

// The response is nested -- Chatwoot wraps the contact inside payload.contact
$contact = $newContact['payload']['contact']   // Primary: nested under payload.contact
    ?? $newContact['contact']                   // Fallback: directly under contact
    ?? $newContact;                              // Fallback: the response itself IS the contact

// The resolved $contact object is expected to have at minimum: id, name, phone_number
// (based on the @return annotation: array{id: int, name: string, phone_number: string})
```

So the Chatwoot `POST /contacts` API returns a structure like:

```json
{
    "payload": {
        "contact": {
            "id": 12345,
            "name": "John Doe",
            "phone_number": "+5511999999999",
            "email": "...",
            "identifier": "...",
            "thumbnail": "...",
            ...
            "contact_inboxes": [
                {
                    "source_id": "...",
                    "inbox": {
                        "id": 1,
                        "name": "WhatsApp",
                        "channel_type": "Channel::Whatsapp"
                    }
                }
            ]
        }
    }
}
```

#### createConversation() Method (lines 2316-2358):

- **Endpoint:** `POST /api/v1/accounts/{accountId}/conversations`
- **Payload:** `{ "contact_id": int, "inbox_id": int }`
- **Returns:** `$response['body']` which contains at least `id` and `display_id` fields

### 4. Existing API Endpoints/Controllers That Create ChatwootContacts From CRM Side

**There are NO existing API endpoints or controllers that create ChatwootContacts from the CRM side.**

Evidence:

- **`ChatwootContact.php` controller** (`/home/antl3x/repos/monostax/mono/components/crm/source/custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Controllers/ChatwootContact.php`) is **completely empty** -- it extends `Base` with no custom actions.

- **`routes.json`** has no routes for ChatwootContact creation. The only ChatwootContact-related route is for `ChatwootContactInbox/:id/createConversation`.

- A `grep` for `createEntity('ChatwootContact'` found only two places, both in **sync jobs** (not controllers):
  1. `/home/antl3x/repos/monostax/mono/components/crm/source/custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Jobs/SyncContactsFromChatwoot.php` line 376
  2. `/home/antl3x/repos/monostax/mono/components/crm/source/custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Jobs/SyncConversationsFromChatwoot.php` line 456

ChatwootContacts are currently created exclusively by background sync jobs pulling data from the Chatwoot API -- never by CRM-side user-facing endpoints.

$data = [

// Teams assignment (required per entityDefs):

#### Chatwoot API field -> EspoCRM field mapping summary:

| Chatwoot API field       | EspoCRM ChatwootContact field | Type              | Required |
|--------------------------|-------------------------------|-------------------|----------|
| `id`                     | `chatwootContactId`           | int               | Yes (unique with account+deleteId) |
| *(EspoCRM link)*         | `chatwootAccountId`           | link (belongsTo)  | Yes (per entityDefs) |
| *(EspoCRM link)*         | `contactId`                   | link (belongsTo)  | No       |
| `name`                   | `name`                        | varchar(255)      | No       |
| `phone_number`           | `phoneNumber`                 | varchar(50)       | No       |
| `email`                  | `email`                       | email             | No       |
| `identifier`             | `identifier`                  | varchar(255)      | No       |
| `availability_status`    | `availabilityStatus`          | enum (online/offline) | No   |
| `blocked`                | `blocked`                     | bool              | No (default false) |
| `thumbnail`              | `avatarUrl`                   | text              | No       |
| `last_activity_at`       | `chatwootLastActivityAt`      | datetime          | No       |
| `created_at`             | `chatwootCreatedAt`           | datetime          | No       |
| *(set programmatically)* | `syncStatus`                  | enum              | No (default "pending") |
| *(set programmatically)* | `lastSyncedAt`                | datetime          | No       |
| *(EspoCRM teams)*        | `teamsIds`                    | linkMultiple      | Yes (per entityDefs, readOnly in UI) |

#### Unique Index Constraint:

From `entityDefs/ChatwootContact.json` (line 182-185):
```json
"chatwootContactIdAccount": {
    "unique": true,
    "columns": ["chatwootContactId", "chatwootAccountId", "deleteId"]
}
```

The `deleteId` column supports soft-deletion -- the combination of `(chatwootContactId, chatwootAccountId, deleteId)` must be unique. The `deleteId` field defaults to `"0"` and is set to the entity ID upon soft-delete.

#### Timestamp Conversion:

Chatwoot sends Unix timestamps (integers), converted via:
```php
private function convertChatwootTimestamp(?int $timestamp): ?string
{
    if ($timestamp === null) return null;
    return date('Y-m-d H:i:s', $timestamp);
}
```

27:     /**

31:      * Returns `null` for unrestricted access (admins), otherwise a concrete allow-list.

35:     public function getAllowedInboxIdList(User $user): ?array
36:     {
37:         if ($user->isAdmin()) {
38:             return null;
39:         }
40: 
41:         $userId = $user->getId();
42: 
43:         if (array_key_exists($userId, $this->allowedInboxIdListByUserId)) {
44:             return $this->allowedInboxIdListByUserId[$userId];
45:         }

50:             ->where([

53:             ->find();
54: 
55:         $inboxIdMap = [];
56: 
57:         foreach ($membershipCollection as $membership) {
58:             if (!$membership instanceof CoreEntity) {
59:                 continue;
60:             }
61: 
62:             foreach ($membership->getLinkMultipleIdList('chatwootInboxes') as $inboxId) {
63:                 $inboxIdMap[$inboxId] = true;
64:             }
65:         }
66: 
67:         $inboxIdList = array_keys($inboxIdMap);

69:         $this->allowedInboxIdListByUserId[$userId] = $inboxIdList;

456:         if (!$phone) {
457:             return null;

460:         // Remove all non-digit characters
461:         $digits = preg_replace('/\D/', '', $phone);
462: 
463:         if (!$digits || strlen($digits) < 10) {
464:             return null;
465:         }
466: 
467:         // Brazilian E.164: 55 + 2-digit DDD + 9-digit mobile (13 digits total)
468:         // Old format with 8-digit mobile (12 digits) needs the "9" prefix added.
469:         if (str_starts_with($digits, '55') && strlen($digits) >= 12 && strlen($digits) <= 13) {
470:             if (strlen($digits) === 12) {
471:                 $ddd = substr($digits, 2, 2);
472:                 $local = substr($digits, 4);
473:                 // Local starts with 6-9 = mobile in old format, add the "9" prefix
474:                 if (preg_match('/^[6-9]/', $local)) {
475:                     $digits = '55' . $ddd . '9' . $local;
476:                 }
477:             }
478:             return '+' . $digits;
479:         }
480: 
481:         // If 10-11 digits, assume Brazilian local number (DDD + local)
482:         if (strlen($digits) >= 10 && strlen($digits) <= 11) {
483:             if (strlen($digits) === 10) {
484:                 $ddd = substr($digits, 0, 2);
485:                 $local = substr($digits, 2);
486:                 if (preg_match('/^[6-9]/', $local)) {
487:                     $digits = $ddd . '9' . $local;
488:                 }
489:             }
490:             return '+55' . $digits;
491:         }
492: 
493:         // Already has country code (other countries)

## Assistant (Research · anthropic/claude-opus-4.6 · 19.8s)

I now have comprehensive knowledge of all components. Here is my complete research report:

---

## Research Summary

The CRM's Chatwoot module has a "Send Message" button on the Contact detail page that opens a channel picker modal. This modal fetches `ChatwootContactInbox` records linked to the Contact — which only exist if the Contact already has a synced `ChatwootContact` in Chatwoot. When no channels exist, a static empty state message is shown ("Nenhum canal de chat disponível"). The user wants to enable conversation initiation from any ChatwootInbox that the logged-in user has access to (via their ChatwootAccountUserMembership), even when the Contact has no pre-existing ChatwootContact link. This requires: (1) the frontend modal showing the user's available inboxes alongside any existing channels, (2) a new backend endpoint that creates a ChatwootContact + ChatwootContactInbox + ChatwootConversation in one operation via the Chatwoot API, and (3) proper phone number normalization since the feature primarily targets WhatsApp inboxes.

## Existing Architecture & Patterns

- **AppParams pattern**: User's Chatwoot context is available client-side via `this.getHelper().getAppParam('chatwootAccountEntityId')` and `getAppParam('chatwootAccountId')`. These are resolved server-side at login through `User → ChatwootUser → ChatwootAccountUserMembership → ChatwootAccount`. (Reference: `custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Classes/AppParams/ChatwootAccountEntityId.php`)

- **Inbox access resolution via membership**: `InboxAccessResolver` is the authoritative mechanism for determining which `ChatwootInbox` IDs a user can access. It queries `ChatwootAccountUserMembership` joined through `ChatwootUser.assignedUserId`, then calls `getLinkMultipleIdList('chatwootInboxes')` on each membership. Admins get `null` (unrestricted). The junction table is `ChatwootAccountUserMembershipChatwootInbox`. (Reference: `custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Tools/Acl/InboxAccessResolver.php`)

- **Existing createConversation endpoint**: `POST ChatwootContactInbox/:id/createConversation` validates access (User → ChatwootUser → membership in account), resolves platform URL/API key (ChatwootAccount → ChatwootPlatform → backendUrl), calls `ChatwootApiClient::createConversation()`, creates local `ChatwootConversation` entity, returns

---

## Research Summary

The CRM's Chatwoot module has a "Send Message" button on the Contact detail page that opens a channel picker modal (`send-message-channel-picker.js`). This modal fetches `ChatwootContactInbox` records for the Contact — which only exist if the Contact already has a synced `ChatwootContact` in Chatwoot. When no `ChatwootContactInbox` records exist, the modal shows a static empty state: "Nenhum canal de chat disponível para este contato". 

The user wants to enable conversation initiation even when the Contact has no pre-existing `ChatwootContact` link. The solution requires: (1) the frontend modal showing the user's available inboxes (from their `ChatwootAccountUserMembership.chatwootInboxes`) as "new conversation" options alongside any existing channels, (2) a new backend endpoint that orchestrates: find/create ChatwootContact in Chatwoot API → create local `ChatwootContact` entity → create local `ChatwootContactInbox` entity → create conversation in Chatwoot API → create local `ChatwootConversation` entity, and (3) proper E.164 phone number normalization for WhatsApp inboxes.

## Existing Architecture & Patterns

- **AppParams pattern**: User's Chatwoot context is available client-side via `this.getHelper().getAppParam(...)`. Key params: `chatwootAccountEntityId` (EspoCRM FK), `chatwootAccountId` (Chatwoot external int), `chatSsoUrl`, `chatwootFrontendUrl`. Resolved at login via `User → ChatwootUser → ChatwootAccountUserMembership → ChatwootAccount`. (Reference: `custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Classes/AppParams/ChatwootAccountEntityId.php`)

- **Inbox access via membership**: `InboxAccessResolver` is the authoritative mechanism. It queries `ChatwootAccountUserMembership` joined through `ChatwootUser.assignedUserId`, then calls `getLinkMultipleIdList('chatwootInboxes')` on each membership. The junction table is `ChatwootAccountUserMembershipChatwootInbox`. Admins get `null` (unrestricted), non-admins get a concrete ID allow-list. (Reference: `custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Tools/Acl/InboxAccessResolver.php`)

- **Existing `createConversation` endpoint**: `POST ChatwootContactInbox/:id/createConversation` validates access (User → ChatwootUser → membership check), resolves platform URL/API key (ChatwootAccount → ChatwootPlatform → `backendUrl`), calls `ChatwootApiClient::createConversation()`, creates local `ChatwootConversation` entity, and returns `{chatwootConversationId, chatwootAccountIdExternal, id}`. (Reference: `custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Controllers/ChatwootContactInbox.php`)

- **Contact creation in Chatwoot API**: `ChatwootApiClient::createContact()` requires `inbox_id` and optionally accepts `phone_number`, `name`. Response is nested: `{payload: {contact: {id, name, phone_number, contact_inboxes: [...]}}}`. The `findOrCreateContact()` method first searches by phone, then creates if not found. (Reference: `custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Services/ChatwootApiClient.php` lines 576, 2268)

- **Phone normalization pattern**: `WhatsAppCampaignService::normalizePhone()` strips non-digits and converts to E.164 format (e.g., `+5511987654321`). Handles Brazilian mobile number formatting including old 8-digit to 9-digit conversion. (Reference: `custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Services/WhatsAppCampaignService.php` line 454)

- **ChatwootContact creation pattern (from sync jobs)**: Creates local entity with fields mapped from Chatwoot API response: `chatwootContactId` (int), `chatwootAccountId` (EspoCRM FK), `contactId` (EspoCRM Contact FK), `name`, `phoneNumber`, `email`, `identifier`, `syncStatus`, `lastSyncedAt`, `teamsIds`. (Reference: `custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Jobs/SyncContactsFromChatwoot.php` line 348)

- **ChatwootContactInbox creation pattern**: Creates with: `chatwootContactId` (EspoCRM entity FK), `contactId` (denormalized EspoCRM Contact FK), `chatwootAccountId`, `chatwootInboxId` (Chatwoot int), `inboxId` (EspoCRM ChatwootInbox FK), `inboxName`, `inboxChannelType` (mapped enum), `sourceId`, `teamsIds`. (Reference: `custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Jobs/SyncContactsFromChatwoot.php` line 622)

- **Channel type mapping**: `SyncContactsFromChatwoot::mapChannelType()` maps Chatwoot's `Channel::Whatsapp` → `whatsapp`, `Channel::Email` → `email`, etc. (Reference: `custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Jobs/SyncContactsFromChatwoot.php` line 648)

## Key Files & Components Found

### Frontend (JavaScript/Template)

- **`client/custom/modules/chatwoot/src/views/contact/modals/send-message-channel-picker.js`**: The modal view. `_loadData()` fetches `ChatwootContactInbox`, `ChatwootConversation`, and `ChatwootInbox` in parallel. `_processData()` merges into `channels[]`. The `_createConversation()` method POSTs to `ChatwootContactInbox/:id/createConversation`. A new section needs to be added: fetching available inboxes from the user's membership and rendering them as "initiate new conversation" options.

- **`client/custom/modules/chatwoot/res/templates/contact/modals/send-message-channel-picker.tpl`**: Handlebars template with the empty state at lines 196-201. Needs a new section for inbox selection when contact has no existing channels, shown alongside existing channels.

- **`client/custom/modules/chatwoot/src/handlers/contact/detail-actions.js`**: Handler that opens the modal. Currently passes `contactId`, `contactName`, `chatwootAccountEntityId`. May need to also pass the Contact's phone number.

### Backend (PHP)

- **`custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Controllers/ChatwootContactInbox.php`**: Existing controller with `postActionCreateConversation()`. A new action is needed (or a new controller) for "initiate conversation on inbox" that handles the full create-contact + create-inbox + create-conversation flow.

- **`custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Services/ChatwootApiClient.php`**: Contains `createContact()` (line 576), `findOrCreateContact()` (line 2268), `createConversation()` (line 2316). All will be used by the new endpoint.

- **`custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Tools/Acl/InboxAccessResolver.php`**: Resolves allowed inbox IDs for a user. Should be reused in the new endpoint for validation.

- **`custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Resources/routes.json`**: Route definitions. A new route needs to be added.

### Entity Definitions

- **`custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Resources/metadata/entityDefs/ChatwootContact.json`**: Unique index on `(chatwootContactId, chatwootAccountId, deleteId)`. Required fields: `chatwootAccount` (link). Has `contact` link (belongsTo Contact), `phoneNumber`, `identifier`, `chatwootContactId` (external int).

- **`custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Resources/metadata/entityDefs/ChatwootContactInbox.json`**: Unique index on `(chatwootContactId, chatwootInboxId, chatwootAccountId, deleteId)`. Required fields: `chatwootContact` (link), `chatwootAccount` (link), `chatwootInboxId` (int). Has `contact` (denormalized), `inbox` (link to ChatwootInbox), `sourceId`, `inboxName`, `inboxChannelType`.

- **`custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Resources/metadata/entityDefs/ChatwootInbox.json`**: Has `channelType` (foreign from ChatwootInboxIntegration), `chatwootInboxId` (external int), `name`, `chatwootAccount` link. The `accountUserMemberships` link enables the many-to-many with memberships.

- **`custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Resources/metadata/entityDefs/ChatwootAccountUserMembership.json`**: Has `chatwootInboxes` linkMultiple (many-to-many with ChatwootInbox via `chatwootAccountUserMembershipChatwootInbox` junction table).

### i18n

- **`custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Resources/i18n/en_US/Contact.json`** and **`pt_BR/Contact.json`**: Already contain labels for "Start Chat", "Start in New Tab", "Creating conversation...", "Failed to create conversation", "No channels available". New labels will be needed (e.g., for inbox selection prompt, "New Conversation" section header).

## Technical Constraints & Considerations

- **No WhatsApp JID/LID fields on Contact**: The Contact entity has no JID or LID fields. The system operates purely on E.164 phone numbers via the `phoneNumber` field (EspoCRM's complex `phone` type backed by a separate `phone_number` table). `$contact->get('phoneNumber')` returns the primary phone number as a string.

- **Phone normalization is required**: The `normalizePhone()` function in `WhatsAppCampaignService` needs to be reused or extracted. It converts raw phone input to E.164 format (`+5511987654321`).

- **Chatwoot `createContact` API response structure**: Nested as `{payload: {contact: {id, name, phone_number, contact_inboxes: [{source_id, inbox: {id, name, channel_type}}]}}}`. The `contact_inboxes` array in the response contains the newly created contact-inbox junction.

- **ChatwootInbox has no selectDefs access control**: Unlike ChatwootConversation/ChatwootContact/ChatwootMessage (which all use `ByMembershipInbox`), the `ChatwootInbox` entity has **no custom selectDefs**. Frontend queries filtering by `chatwootAccountId` will return all inboxes for that account if the user has team-based ACL read access. The backend new endpoint must explicitly validate inbox access using `InboxAccessResolver::canAccessInboxId()`.

- **Existing channels must coexist with new inbox options**: Per user requirement, the modal should show both existing `ChatwootContactInbox` channels (with their existing conversations) AND available inboxes that don't yet have a ContactInbox for this Contact. The frontend needs to deduplicate — an inbox already represented by a ContactInbox should not appear twice.

- **The `ChatwootContact.chatwootContactId` comes from the Chatwoot API response**: When creating a contact via `createContact()`, the API response includes `id` which becomes `chatwootContactId` on the local entity. This must be set correctly to maintain sync integrity.

- **Unique constraint considerations**: `ChatwootContact` has a unique index on `(chatwootContactId, chatwootAccountId, deleteId)`. `ChatwootContactInbox` has a unique index on `(chatwootContactId, chatwootInboxId, chatwootAccountId, deleteId)`. The new endpoint must handle the case where the contact already exists in Chatwoot (phone match) but doesn't have a local `ChatwootContact` entity yet.

- **Teams assignment**: All new entities (`ChatwootContact`, `ChatwootContactInbox`, `ChatwootConversation`) require `teamsIds`. The pattern is: try from the inbox entity's teams, fall back to the ChatwootAccount's teams.

## Unanswered Questions

None — all ambiguities have been clarified by the user.

## Readiness for Planning

**Status:** READY FOR PLANNING

171:             conversations.forEach(function (conv) {
172:                 var key = conv.contactInboxId;
173:                 if (key && !conversationMap[key]) {
174:                     conversationMap[key] = conv;
175:                 }
176:             });
177: 
178:             // Map ChatwootInbox by id for channelType lookup
179:             var inboxMap = {};
180:             chatwootInboxes.forEach(function (cInbox) {
181:                 inboxMap[cInbox.id] = cInbox;
182:             });
183: 
184:             this.channels = contactInboxes.map(
185:                 function (contactInbox) {
186:                     var conversation = conversationMap[contactInbox.id] || null;
187: 
188:                     // Resolve channelType from linked ChatwootInbox
189:                     // (which is a foreign field from ChatwootInboxIntegration)
190:                     var chatwootInbox = contactInbox.inboxId ? inboxMap[contactInbox.inboxId] : null;
191:                     var integrationChannelType = chatwootInbox ? chatwootInbox.channelType : null;
192: 
193:                     var iconInfo = this._getChannelIcon(integrationChannelType);
194: 
195:                     return {
196:                         contactInboxId: contactInbox.id,
197:                         inboxName: contactInbox.inboxName || "Unknown Channel",
198:                         inboxChannelType: contactInbox.inboxChannelType,
199:                         channelTypeLabel: iconInfo.label,
200:                         svgIconUrl: iconInfo.svgIconUrl,
201:                         iconClass: iconInfo.iconClass,
202:                         hasConversation: !!conversation,
203:                         chatwootConversationId: conversation
204:                             ? conversation.chatwootConversationId
205:                             : null,
206:                         chatwootAccountIdExternal: conversation
207:                             ? conversation.chatwootAccountIdExternal
208:                             : null,
209:                         conversationEntityId: conversation
210:                             ? conversation.id
211:                             : null,
212:                     };
213:                 }.bind(this)
214:             );
215:         },
216: 
217:         /**
218:          * Get the icon for a channel using the ChatwootInboxIntegration channelType.
219:          *
220:          * Uses the same resolution logic as the svg-icon-enum field view
221:          * on the ChatwootInbox sidepanel:
222:          *   1. svgIconByValue exact match (e.g. "whatsappQrcode" → "whatsapp")
223:          *   2. pickByContains fallback (e.g. value contains "whatsapp" → "whatsapp")
224:          *   3. Font Awesome fallback for unknown types
225:          *
226:          * @param {string|null} channelType - ChatwootInboxIntegration.channelType (e.g. "whatsappQrcode", "whatsappCloudApi")
227:          * @returns {{ svgIconUrl: string|null, iconClass: string|null, label: string }}
228:          */
229:         _getChannelIcon: function (channelType) {
230:             // Exact value map — mirrors entityDefs ChatwootInbox.channelType.svgIconByValue
231:             var svgIconByValue = {
232:                 whatsappQrcode: "whatsapp",
233:                 whatsappCloudApi: "whatsapp",
234:             };
235: 
236:             // Contains-based fallback — mirrors svg-icon-enum.js pickByContains
237:             var containsMap = {
238:                 whatsapp: "whatsapp",
239:                 waha: "whatsapp",
240:                 telegram: "telegram",
241:                 instagram: "instagram",
242:                 facebook: "messenger",
243:                 messenger: "messenger",
244:             };
245: 
246:             // Icon name → SVG file — mirrors SVG_ICON_FILE_MAP in svg-icon-enum.js
247:             var svgFileMap = {
248:                 whatsapp: "whatsapp.svg",
249:                 telegram: "telegram.svg",
250:                 instagram: "instagram.svg",
251:                 messenger: "messenger.svg",
252:             };
253: 
254:             // Font Awesome fallback for channels without SVG icons
255:             var faIconMap = {
256:                 email: "fas fa-envelope",
257:                 web: "fas fa-globe",
258:                 sms: "fas fa-sms",
259:                 api: "fas fa-plug",
260:                 default: "fas fa-comment",
261:             };
262: 
263:             var value = channelType || "";
264:             var iconName = null;
265: 
266:             // Step 1: Exact match (svgIconByValue)
267:             iconName = svgIconByValue[value] || null;
268: 
269:             // Step 2: Contains-based fallback
270:             if (!iconName && value) {
271:                 var normalized = value.toLowerCase();
272:                 for (var needle in containsMap) {
273:                     if (normalized.includes(needle)) {
274:                         iconName = containsMap[needle];
275:                         break;
276:                     }
277:                 }
278:             }
279: 
280:             // Step 3: Resolve SVG file
281:             var svgFile = iconName ? svgFileMap[iconName] : null;
282: 
283:             if (svgFile) {
284:                 return {
285:                     svgIconUrl: this.getBasePath() + "client/custom/modules/global/res/icons/" + svgFile,
286:                     iconClass: null,
287:                     label: iconName,
288:                 };
289:             }
290: 
291:             // Step 4: Font Awesome fallback
292:             var label = value ? value.toLowerCase() : "default";
293: 
294:             return {
295:                 svgIconUrl: null,
296:                 iconClass: faIconMap[label] || faIconMap["default"],
297:                 label: label,
298:             };
299:         },
300: 
301:         /**
302:          * Get a channel object from the channels array by its template index.
303:          */
304:         _getChannelByIndex: function (index) {
305:             return this.channels[index] || null;
306:         },
307: 
308:         /**
309:          * Build the cwPath for a Chatwoot conversation URL.
310:          */
311:         _buildConversationPath: function (chatwootAccountId, chatwootConversationId) {
312:             return (
313:                 "/app/accounts/" +
314:                 chatwootAccountId +
315:                 "/inbox-view/conversation/" +
316:                 chatwootConversationId
317:             );
318:         },
319: 
320:         /**
321:          * Open a conversation in a new tab using the SSO-authenticated
322:          * #Chatwoot?cwPath= route.
323:          */
324:         _openInNewTab: function (chatwootAccountId, chatwootConversationId) {
325:             var cwPath = this._buildConversationPath(
326:                 chatwootAccountId,
327:                 chatwootConversationId
328:             );
329:             window.open("#Chatwoot?cwPath=" + cwPath, "_blank");
330:         },
331: 
332:         /**
333:          * Open a conversation in the iframe drawer modal.
334:          * Follows the kanban-item.js actionQuickView() pattern.
335:          */
336:         _openInDrawer: function (
337:             chatwootConversationId,
338:             chatwootAccountIdExternal,
339:             conversationEntityId
340:         ) {
341:             this.createView(
342:                 "conversationDrawer",
343:                 "chatwoot:views/chatwoot-conversation/modals/conversation-drawer",
344:                 {
345:                     chatwootConversationId: chatwootConversationId,
346:                     chatwootAccountIdExternal: chatwootAccountIdExternal,
347:                     contactName: this.contactName,
348:                     recordId: conversationEntityId,
349:                 },
350:                 function (view) {
351:                     view.render();
352:                 }
353:             );
354:         },
355: 
356:         /**
357:          * Create a new conversation for a ContactInbox, then invoke the callback
358:          * with the created conversation data.
359:          *
360:          * Shows a loading spinner on the channel row during creation.
361:          */
362:         _createConversation: function (index, callback) {
363:             var channel = this._getChannelByIndex(index);
364:             if (!channel) return;
365: 
366:             // Show loading state on the row
367:             var $row = this.$el.find('.channel-item[data-index="' + index + '"]');
368:             $row.addClass("is-creating");
369: 
370:             Espo.Ajax.postRequest(
371:                 "ChatwootContactInbox/" + channel.contactInboxId + "/createConversation"
372:             )
373:                 .then(
374:                     function (response) {
375:                         // Update the channel data with the new conversation
376:                         channel.hasConversation = true;
377:                         channel.chatwootConversationId =
378:                             response.chatwootConversationId;
379:                         channel.chatwootAccountIdExternal =
380:                             response.chatwootAccountIdExternal;
381:                         channel.conversationEntityId = response.id;
382: 
383:                         $row.removeClass("is-creating");
384: 
385:                         if (callback) {
386:                             callback(response);
387:                         }
388:                     }.bind(this)
389:                 )
390:                 .catch(
391:                     function (xhr) {
392:                         $row.removeClass("is-creating");
393: 
394:                         var errorMsg = this.translate(
395:                             "Failed to create conversation",
396:                             "labels",
397:                             "Contact"
398:                         );
399:                         if (xhr && xhr.responseJSON && xhr.responseJSON.message) {
400:                             errorMsg = xhr.responseJSON.message;
401:                         }
402: 
403:                         Espo.Ui.error(errorMsg);
404:                     }.bind(this)
405:                 );
406:         },
407: 
408:         /**
409:          * Handle "Open in New Tab" action.
410:          * If no conversation exists, creates one first.
411:          */
412:         actionOpenTab: function (data) {
413:             var index = parseInt(data.index, 10);
414:             var channel = this._getChannelByIndex(index);
415:             if (!channel) return;
416: 
417:             if (channel.hasConversation) {
418:                 this._openInNewTab(
419:                     channel.chatwootAccountIdExternal || this.chatwootAccountId,
420:                     channel.chatwootConversationId
421:                 );
422:                 this.close();
423:             } else {
424:                 this._createConversation(
425:                     index,
426:                     function (response) {
427:                         this._openInNewTab(
428:                             response.chatwootAccountIdExternal || this.chatwootAccountId,
429:                             response.chatwootConversationId
430:                         );
431:                         this.close();
432:                     }.bind(this)
433:                 );
434:             }
435:         },
436: 
437:         /**
438:          * Handle "Open in Drawer" action.
439:          * If no conversation exists, creates one first.
440:          */
441:         actionOpenDrawer: function (data) {
442:             var index = parseInt(data.index, 10);
443:             var channel = this._getChannelByIndex(index);
444:             if (!channel) return;
445: 
446:             if (channel.hasConversation) {
447:                 this.close();
448:                 this._openInDrawer(
449:                     channel.chatwootConversationId,
450:                     channel.chatwootAccountIdExternal || this.chatwootAccountId,
451:                     channel.conversationEntityId
452:                 );
453:             } else {
454:                 this._createConversation(
455:                     index,
456:                     function (response) {
457:                         this.close();
458:                         this._openInDrawer(
459:                             response.chatwootConversationId,
460:                             response.chatwootAccountIdExternal || this.chatwootAccountId,
461:                             response.id
462:                         );
463:                     }.bind(this)
464:                 );
465:             }
466:         },
467: 
468:         actionClose: function () {
469:             this.close();
470:         },
471:     });
472: });

141: <div class="channel-picker-modal">

18: 
19:     return class {
20:         constructor(view) {

23: 
24:         /**

26:          * (meaning they have a linked ChatwootUser with valid SSO).
27:          * This is synchronous — no API call needed.
28:          */
29:         isSendMessageAvailable() {
30:             return !!this.view.getHelper().getAppParam('chatSsoUrl');

32:

566:     /**
567:      * Create a contact on Chatwoot via Account API.
568:      *
569:      * @param string $platformUrl The base URL of the Chatwoot platform
570:      * @param string $accountApiKey The account-level API key
571:      * @param int $accountId The Chatwoot account ID
572:      * @param array<string, mixed> $contactData Contact data to send
573:      * @return array<string, mixed> Response data from Chatwoot API
574:      * @throws Error
575:      */
576:     public function createContact(
577:         string $platformUrl,
578:         string $accountApiKey,
579:         int $accountId,
580:         array $contactData
581:     ): array {
582:         // Validate required field
583:         if (!isset($contactData['inbox_id'])) {
584:             throw new Error('inbox_id is required to create a contact in Chatwoot.');
585:         }
586:         
587:         $url = rtrim($platformUrl, '/') . '/api/v1/accounts/' . $accountId . '/contacts';
588:         
589:         $payload = json_encode($contactData);
590:         
591:         if ($payload === false) {
592:             throw new Error('Failed to encode contact data to JSON.');

594: 
595:         $headers = [
596:             'api_access_token: ' . $accountApiKey,
597:             'Content-Type: application/json',
598:             'Content-Length: ' . strlen($payload)
599:         ];
600: 
601:         $response = $this->executeRequest($url, 'POST', $payload, $headers);
602: 
603:         if ($response['code'] < 200 || $response['code'] >= 300) {
604:             $errorMsg = 'Chatwoot API error: HTTP ' . $response['code'];
605:             
606:             if (isset($response['body']['message'])) {

608:             } elseif (isset($response['body']['error'])) {
609:                 $errorMsg .= ' - ' . $response['body']['error'];
610:             }
611:             
612:             $this->log->error('Chatwoot API Error (createContact): ' . json_encode($response));
613:             throw new Error($errorMsg);
614:         }
615: 
616:         return $response['body'];
617:     }

754:      *

756:      * @param string $accountApiKey The account-level API key

762:     public function searchContactByPhone(
763:         string $platformUrl,

767:     ): ?array {

## Assistant (Scope · anthropic/claude-opus-4.6 · 99.9s)

Now I have everything I need. Let me write the scope document.

---

## Decisions

| # | Decision | Alternatives Considered | Rationale |
|---|----------|------------------------|-----------|
| 1 | New backend controller `Contact` (in the Chatwoot module) with a `postActionInitiateConversation` action, rather than extending the existing `ChatwootContactInbox` controller | Extending `ChatwootContactInbox::postActionCreateConversation` with optional body params; creating a standalone service class | The existing `createConversation` operates on a known `ChatwootContactInbox` entity (by ID). The new flow starts from an EspoCRM Contact + a ChatwootInbox — fundamentally different entry point. A separate action on the Contact controller keeps responsibilities clean, and the route reads naturally: `POST Contact/:id/initiateConversation`. |
| 2 | Extract `normalizePhone()` into a shared utility class `PhoneNormalizer` | Duplicate inline in the new controller; call `WhatsAppCampaignService` as a dependency | Phone normalization is already duplicated in `WhatsAppCampaignService` and `SyncContactsFromSimplesAgenda`. A shared utility follows DRY and is testable in isolation. Both existing callers should be updated to use it. |
| 3 | Backend validates inbox access using `InboxAccessResolver::canAccessInboxId()` | Re-implementing the User→ChatwootUser→Membership lookup manually | The `InboxAccessResolver` already encapsulates the exact authorization logic, caches results per-user, and is used by all access control filters. Reusing it ensures consistency. |
| 4 | Reuse `ChatwootApiClient::findOrCreateContact()` to handle both "contact exists in Chatwoot" and "contact doesn't exist" cases | Calling `searchContactByPhone` + `createContact` manually in the controller | `findOrCreateContact` already encodes the exact search-then-create logic, handles the nested response structure, and is battle-tested by the WhatsApp campaign flow. |
| 5 | The frontend shows available inboxes as a separate "New Conversation" section below existing channels, not mixed into the same list | Interleaving new-inbox rows with existing-channel rows; showing inboxes only when zero channels exist | Per user requirement, both must coexist. A visual separator makes it clear which options create net-new Chatwoot entities vs. which reuse existing channels. Deduplication filters out inboxes that already have a `ChatwootContactInbox` for this Contact. |
| 6 | The frontend fetches available inboxes by querying `ChatwootInbox` filtered by `chatwootAccountId` and relying on standard team-based ACL | Adding a new AppParam for the user's inbox list; querying the membership's `chatwootInboxes` directly from JS | `ChatwootInbox` already uses team-based ACL. Since the `SyncInboxMembersFromChatwoot` job ensures the membership-inbox junction table is synchronized, and the inbox's teams mirror the account's teams, team-based ACL is sufficient for listing. The backend endpoint performs the authoritative `InboxAccessResolver` check anyway. |
| 7 | Backend creates local `ChatwootContact` + `ChatwootContactInbox` + `ChatwootConversation` entities in one transactional flow | Creating only the conversation and relying on the sync job to backfill the contact/inbox entities | The sync job runs on a schedule. The user expects to see the conversation immediately after clicking "Start Chat". Creating all entities synchronously ensures the conversation drawer can open instantly and the Contact's relationship panels reflect the new data. |
| 8 | The Contact's primary `phoneNumber` is used as the identifier, not asking the user to input a phone number | Showing a phone input field in the modal | The Contact already has a `phoneNumber` field. For WhatsApp inboxes, this is the natural identifier. If the Contact has no phone number, the inbox option should be hidden or disabled for WhatsApp-type inboxes. This avoids adding UI complexity. |

## File Manifest

### Files to CREATE (ordered by complexity/risk, highest first)

---

#### 1. `custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Controllers/ContactChatwoot.php`

**Purpose:** New controller with `postActionInitiateConversation` — the core backend endpoint for initiating a conversation on a ChatwootInbox for a Contact that may or may not have a linked ChatwootContact.

**Why complex:** This is the heart of the feature. It orchestrates a multi-step flow: validate user access → resolve platform credentials → find-or-create Chatwoot contact → create local ChatwootContact entity → create local ChatwootContactInbox entity → create Chatwoot conversation → create local ChatwootConversation entity. Must handle idempotency (contact might already exist in Chatwoot), error recovery, and entity relationship integrity.

**Key design:**
- Route: `POST Contact/:id/initiateConversation` with request body `{ "inboxId": "<EspoCRM ChatwootInbox entity ID>" }`
- Follow the access validation pattern from `ChatwootContactInbox::postActionCreateConversation`:
  - Resolve `User → ChatwootUser → ChatwootAccountUserMembership` 
  - Then use `InboxAccessResolver::canAccessInboxId()` to validate the user can access the target inbox
- Resolve platform URL/API key via `ChatwootInbox → chatwootAccountId → ChatwootAccount → platformId → ChatwootPlatform → backendUrl` (same chain as the existing controller)
- Get the Contact's phone number via `$contact->get('phoneNumber')`, normalize it using the new `PhoneNormalizer` utility
- Call `ChatwootApiClient::findOrCreateContact($platformUrl, $accountApiKey, $externalAccountId, $externalInboxId, $normalizedPhone, $contactName)`
- Create local `ChatwootContact` entity following the field mapping pattern in `SyncContactsFromChatwoot::createNewContact()` — but check first if one already exists for this `(chatwootContactId, chatwootAccountId)` pair (including soft-deleted via `withDeleted()` + `restoreDeleted()`)
- Create local `ChatwootContactInbox` entity following the pattern in `SyncContactsFromChatwoot::syncContactInboxes()` — check for existing first too
- Call `ChatwootApiClient::createConversation()` and create local `ChatwootConversation` following the exact pattern from `ChatwootContactInbox::postActionCreateConversation` lines 163-201
- Return same response shape: `{ chatwootConversationId, chatwootAccountIdExternal, id }`
- Teams: resolve from ChatwootInbox teams, fall back to ChatwootAccount teams

**Reference files to follow:**
- `custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Controllers/ChatwootContactInbox.php` — access validation pattern, entity creation, response shape
- `custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Jobs/SyncContactsFromChatwoot.php` lines 348-387 — `ChatwootContact` creation field mapping
- `custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Jobs/SyncContactsFromChatwoot.php` lines 620-641 — `ChatwootContactInbox` creation field mapping
- `custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Jobs/SyncConversationsFromChatwoot.php` lines 555-608 — `ChatwootContactInbox` creation pattern with channel type mapping

**Dependencies:** `InboxAccessResolver`, `ChatwootApiClient`, `PhoneNormalizer` (new), `EntityManager`

---

#### 2. `custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Tools/PhoneNormalizer.php`

**Purpose:** Shared utility that normalizes raw phone number strings to E.164 format. Extracted from the duplicated `normalizePhone()` private methods.

**Why it exists:** Currently duplicated in `WhatsAppCampaignService` (line 454) and `SyncContactsFromSimplesAgenda` (line 508). The new controller also needs it. Extract into a stateless utility with a single `public static function normalize(?string $phone): ?string` method.

**Implementation:** Copy the logic verbatim from `WhatsAppCampaignService::normalizePhone()` — handles Brazilian E.164 conversion, old 8-digit mobile format, country code detection.

**Reference file:** `custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Services/WhatsAppCampaignService.php` lines 454-499

#### 3. `client/custom/modules/chatwoot/src/views/contact/modals/send-message-channel-picker.js`

**Purpose:** Extend the channel picker modal to also show available inboxes (from the user's ChatwootAccount) as "new conversation" options alongside existing channels.

**What to change:**

- **In `_loadData()`**: Add a 4th parallel API request to fetch `ChatwootInbox` records that the user can access. The existing `chatwootInboxPromise` already fetches all inboxes for the account — this same data can be reused. But we also need the `name` field of each inbox (currently only `id,channelType` is selected). Change the `select` on the existing `chatwootInboxPromise` to also include `name,chatwootInboxId`.

- **In `_processData()`**: After building the `channels` array from `contactInboxes`, compute an `availableInboxes` array from the `chatwootInboxes` that do **not** already have a corresponding `ChatwootContactInbox` for this Contact. Deduplication: build a `Set` of `inboxId` values (the EspoCRM ChatwootInbox entity IDs) from the `contactInboxes`, then filter `chatwootInboxes` to exclude those already represented. For each remaining inbox, build an object similar to the channel items but with `isNewInbox: true`, `inboxEntityId` (the EspoCRM ChatwootInbox ID), and no conversation data.

- **In `data()`**: Add `hasAvailableInboxes` and `availableInboxes` to the template data.

- **Add `_initiateConversation(inboxIndex, callback)` method**: New method that POSTs to `Contact/<contactId>/initiateConversation` with body `{ inboxId: "<ChatwootInbox entity ID>" }`. Shows loading state on the inbox row. On success, receives the same response shape as `_createConversation`. Follow the same loading-spinner / error-handling pattern as `_createConversation`.

- **Add `actionOpenTabNewInbox(data)` and `actionOpenDrawerNewInbox(data)` actions**: New action handlers for the "new inbox" rows. These call `_initiateConversation()` then open tab/drawer on success.

- **Update `hasChannels` logic**: Change the empty state condition. The empty state (the "no channels" message) should now only show when BOTH `channels` is empty AND `availableInboxes` is empty. If there are available inboxes but no existing channels, show the inbox section. If there are existing channels, always show them regardless.

- **Pass `contactPhoneNumber`**: Read from `this.options.contactPhoneNumber` (passed by the detail-actions handler). Disable/hide WhatsApp-type inboxes that require a phone number when the Contact has no phone number.

**Reference files:**
- Current implementation in the same file — follow the exact same patterns for API calls, loading states, error handling
- `client/custom/modules/chatwoot/src/views/chatwoot-conversation/modals/conversation-drawer.js` — for how to open the drawer after creation

### Files to CONSIDER

- **`custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Services/WhatsAppCampaignService.php`** — The `normalizePhone()` method (line 454) should be updated to delegate to the new `PhoneNormalizer` utility. Not strictly required for this feature but prevents further drift.

- **`custom/Espo/Modules/FeatureIntegrationSimplesAgenda/Jobs/SyncContactsFromSimplesAgenda.php`** — Also has a duplicated `normalizePhone()` (line 508). Same consideration as above.

- **`custom/Espo/Modules/Chatwoot/Resources/metadata/selectDefs/ChatwootInbox.json`** — Currently does not exist. `ChatwootInbox` has no `ByMembershipInbox` access control filter (unlike `ChatwootConversation`, `ChatwootContact`, `ChatwootMessage`). The frontend query for available inboxes relies on standard team ACL. If team ACL is insufficient (e.g., user can see inboxes they don't have membership for), a new `selectDefs/ChatwootInbox.json` with a `ByMembershipInbox` filter would need to be created. The backend endpoint already uses `InboxAccessResolver` for authorization, so the risk is limited to the frontend showing inboxes the user technically can't use (which would then fail on the backend). Worth evaluating after initial implementation.
